# Python API Reference

## Main classes

### TextEmbedding

Generates dense vector embeddings from text.

#### Constructor

```python
TextEmbedding(
    model_name="BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5",
    provider="cpu",
    threads=0,
    batch_size=256,
    offline=False,
    show_download_progress=True,
    cache_dir=None,
    max_length=0,
    dim=0,
    pooling="mean",
    auto_workers=False,
    cache_size=0,
    quantization=None,
    truncate_dim=None,
    num_threads=None,  # deprecated
)
```

**Parameters:**

| Parameter | Type | Default | Description |
|-----------|------|---------|-------------|
| `model_name` | `str` | `"BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5"` | HuggingFace model name, ONNX repo code, or local directory path containing `model.onnx` + `tokenizer.json` |
| `provider` | `str` | `"cpu"` | Execution provider: `"cpu"`, `"coreml"` (Apple), `"llamacpp"`; `"cuda"`, `"directml"`, `"tensorrt"` are accepted but run on CPU (GPU planned after 1.x) |
| `threads` | `int` | `0` | Number of threads (`0` = auto) |
| `batch_size` | `int` | `256` | Internal batch size for inference |
| `offline` | `bool` | `False` | `True` = use cache only, never download |
| `show_download_progress` | `bool` | `True` | Show download progress bar |
| `cache_dir` | `str \| None` | `None` | Model cache directory (`None` = `~/.cache/libembedding`) |
| `max_length` | `int` | `0` | Max token length (`0` = model default) |
| `dim` | `int` | `0` | Embedding dimension for local models without `config.json` |
| `pooling` | `str` | `"mean"` | Pooling strategy for local models: `"cls"` or `"mean"` |
| `auto_workers` | `bool` | `False` | `True` = auto-detect optimal sessions/workers for llama.cpp backend |
| `cache_size` | `int` | `0` | LRU embedding cache size (`0` = disabled) |
| `quantization` | `str \| None` | `None` | Quantization mode: `"none"`, `"static"`, `"dynamic"`, `"fp16"` (None = model default). **Selects the registry entry providing that mode**, i.e. a different set of weights — it is not a session option. A mode the registry does not offer for this model raises `ModelNotFoundError` naming the modes that are available. ⚠️ `"auto"` is accepted by this parameter but **selects no weights**: it loads the default entry (FP32) and the `.quantization` property reports `"auto"`. For a measured auto-selection, use `preferred_quantization="auto"`. ⚠️ `"fp16"` is not INT8: those are `FLOAT16` weights served by an ORT-optimized graph. On a CPU without native FP16 arithmetic they are slower than FP32 while occupying less disk — useful for size, not for throughput. The four `Qdrant/*-onnx-Q` entries (including `bge-small-en-v1.5`) fall into that case; their `_Q` suffix and their historical "Quantized" description did not change that. |
| `preferred_quantization` | `str \| None` | `None` | `"auto"` = **measured selection**: the variants the registry actually ships for this model are benchmarked once per (model, machine, library version), the winner is cached, and later loads read the cache. Rule: no measurable FP32 baseline → FP32; a variant must beat FP32 by **more than 5 %**; and **dynamic INT8 is preferred** — another variant must beat it by **more than 15 %**. Cost: ~3.5 s on the very first load of a model, then free. See [Quantization](#quantization--fp32-vs-int8) for the measurements. |
| `truncate_dim` | `int \| None` | `None` | [Matryoshka](matryoshka.md) truncation: keeps the first `truncate_dim` components of every vector, then L2-renormalises them. Between `1` and the native dimension (otherwise `ValueError`). Meant for Matryoshka-trained models (`nomic-embed-text-v1.5`, `mxbai-embed-large-v1`, `embeddinggemma-300m`). |
| `num_threads` | `int \| None` | `None` | **Deprecated** — use `threads` instead |

#### Methods and properties

| Member | Type | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `embed(texts, batch_size=None)` | `np.ndarray` | Embed texts. Returns array of shape `(n, dim)` with `float32` dtype. L2-normalized. |
| `embed_stream(texts, callback, batch_size=None)` | `None` | Embed as a stream — calls `callback(array, dim, userdata)` for each embedding |
| `embed_batched(texts, batch_size=None)` | `Generator` | Embed in batches, yielding an array per batch |
| `dim` | `int` (property) | Dimension of the returned vectors (`truncate_dim` when set) |
| `native_dim` | `int` (property) | Dimension produced by the model, before truncation |
| `truncate_dim` | `int \| None` (property) | Matryoshka truncation size, `None` when vectors are kept whole |
| `quantization` | `str` (property) | Quantization mode actually in use (`"none"`, `"static"`, `"dynamic"`, `"fp16"`). With `preferred_quantization="auto"`, reports the mode that was selected. |
| `quantization_reason` | `str` (property) | Why `preferred_quantization="auto"` picked that mode, e.g. `"1.69x the fp32 variant (15 -> 26 docs/s)"`. Empty when nothing was measured (explicit mode, or a model with no quantized sibling). |
| `batch_size` | `int` (property) | Configured batch size |
| `name` | `str` (property) | Model name or local path |
| `model_name` | `str` (property) | Registry name of the loaded model |
| `backend` | `str` (property) | Runtime actually serving the model: `"onnxruntime"` or `"llama.cpp"` (see [Effective backend and provider](#effective-backend-and-provider)) |
| `provider` | `str` (property) | Execution provider actually in use, lowercase: `"cpu"`, `"cuda"`, `"coreml"`, `"directml"`, `"tensorrt"`; `"gpu"` for llama.cpp with offloaded layers |
| `info()` | `ModelDesc` | Loaded model descriptor |
| `stats()` | `Stats` | Runtime usage statistics |
| `close()` | `None` | Release resources |
| `__enter__()` | `self` | Context manager support |
| `__exit__()` | `None` | Calls `close()` automatically |
| `from_mode(mode="balanced", **kwargs)` | `TextEmbedding` | (classmethod) Create a TextEmbedding from a preset mode: `"fast"`, `"balanced"`, `"quality"` |

> **No `list_supported_models()` on `TextEmbedding`** — it only exists on
> `SparseTextEmbedding`, `ImageEmbedding` and `Reranker`. For text models use the
> module-level `list_text_models()`.

> **No `from_gguf()` and no `supports_llamacpp()`.** GGUF loading goes through
> the constructor: `_is_gguf_model()` recognises the `.gguf` extension and
> switches to the llama.cpp backend. For a HuggingFace repo pass
> `"repo/filename.gguf"`; for a local file pass the path.
> `detect_backend(model_name)` reports the backend that was selected.

#### Example

```python
from libembedding import TextEmbedding
import numpy as np

model = TextEmbedding("BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")
embeddings = model.embed(["Hello world", "How are you?"])
print(embeddings.shape)   # (2, 384)
print(embeddings.dtype)   # float32

# Simple semantic search
query = model.embed(["What is AI?"])
scores = embeddings @ query.T
best_idx = scores.argmax()
```

---

### SparseTextEmbedding

Generates **sparse** embeddings (sparse vectors with token indices and weights).
`batch_size=None` uses the backend default: 256 for ONNX and 16 for GGUF. The
GGUF default is a measured compromise between throughput, stability and number
of batches; batch 2 can further reduce padding and memory at similar throughput.
Callers can override either setting.

#### Constructor

```python
SparseTextEmbedding(
    model_name="prithivida/Splade_PP_en_v1",
    provider="cpu",
    device_id=0,
    cache_dir=None,
    max_length=0,
    threads=0,
    batch_size=None,
    offline=False,
    show_download_progress=True,
    top_terms=0,
    min_weight=0.0,
    quantization=None,
    backend="auto",
    cache_size=0,
    num_threads=None,  # deprecated
)
```

**Available models:**

| HuggingFace name | Description |
|------------------|-------------|
| `prithivida/Splade_PP_en_v1` | SPLADE++ (default) |
| `BAAI/bge-m3` | Multilingual BGE-M3 |

#### Methods and properties

| Member | Type | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `embed(texts, batch_size=0)` | `list[SparseEmbedding]` | Returns a list of `SparseEmbedding` objects |
| `batch_size` | `int` (property) | Configured batch size |
| `name` | `str` (property) | Model name |
| `backend` | `str` (property) | `"onnxruntime"` or `"ggml-sparse"` (the library's own ggml runtime for GGUF SPLADE models, not llama.cpp) |
| `provider` | `str` (property) | Execution provider actually in use (`"cpu"` for `"ggml-sparse"`) |
| `info()` | `ModelDesc` | Model descriptor |
| `stats()` | `Stats` | Runtime usage statistics |
| `close()` | `None` | Release resources |
| `list_supported_models()` | `list[ModelInfo]` | (static) List sparse models |

#### Example

```python
from libembedding import SparseTextEmbedding

sparse = SparseTextEmbedding()
results = sparse.embed(["machine learning algorithms"])

for r in results:
    print(r.indices.shape, r.values.shape)
    # indices: int32 array of token IDs
    # values: float32 array of weights
```

### ImageEmbedding

Generates dense vector embeddings from images.

#### Constructor

```python
ImageEmbedding(
    model_name="Qdrant/clip-ViT-B-32-vision",
    provider="cpu",
    device_id=0,
    cache_dir=None,
    threads=0,
    batch_size=30,
    offline=False,
    show_download_progress=True,
    dim=0,
    num_threads=None,  # deprecated
)
```

#### Methods and properties

| Member | Type | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `embed_files(paths, batch_size=None)` | `np.ndarray` | Embed from file paths. Shape: `(n, dim)` |
| `embed_bytes(images, batch_size=None)` | `np.ndarray` | Embed from raw bytes (JPEG, PNG, etc.) |
| `dim` | `int` (property) | Embedding dimension |
| `batch_size` | `int` (property) | Configured batch size |
| `name` | `str` (property) | Model name |
| `backend` | `str` (property) | Always `"onnxruntime"` |
| `provider` | `str` (property) | Execution provider actually in use |
| `info()` | `ModelDesc` | Model descriptor |
| `stats()` | `Stats` | Runtime usage statistics |
| `close()` | `None` | Release resources |
| `list_supported_models()` | `list[ModelInfo]` | (static) List image models |

#### Example

```python
from libembedding import ImageEmbedding

model = ImageEmbedding()
embeddings = model.embed_files(["photo.jpg", "diagram.png"])
print(embeddings.shape)  # (2, 512)
```

---

### MultiVectorEmbedding

Multi-vector (late interaction, ColBERT) embeddings: one float32 matrix
`[n_tokens, dim]` per text. ONNX Runtime only. The model is either a **local
directory** (`model.onnx` or `vespa_colbert.onnx`, `tokenizer.json`, and an optional
`onnx_config.json`) or a **registry entry**
(`"answerdotai/answerai-colbert-small-v1"`, downloaded into the cache on first use;
an existing directory wins). The library embeds; it does not index or search: score with
`maxsim` / `maxsim_batch`. Alias: `LateInteractionTextEmbedding` (FastEmbed
naming). C API: `multivector_embedding.h` (`lembed_multivector_*`).

#### Constructor

```python
MultiVectorEmbedding(
    model_dir, *, provider="cpu", threads=0, batch_size=0,
    query_maxlen=None, doc_maxlen=None, normalize=True,
    query_expansion=None, attend_to_expansion=None,
    cache_dir=None, offline=False, show_download_progress=False,
)
```

`cache_dir` / `offline` / `show_download_progress`: registry models only.
`MultiVectorEmbedding.list_supported_models()` lists the registry.
`query_maxlen` / `doc_maxlen`: length overrides (model defaults: 32 / 300).
`query_expansion`: pad queries with `[MASK]` (`None` = on).
`attend_to_expansion`: `None` = follow the model.

#### Methods and properties

| Member | Type | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `embed(texts, *, mode="document")` | `list[np.ndarray]` | One `[n_i, dim]` matrix per text; `mode` is `"document"` or `"query"` |
| `query_embed(query)` | `Iterator[np.ndarray]` | Query or queries (`str` or list), `query` mode |
| `passage_embed(texts)` | `Iterator[np.ndarray]` | Documents, `document` mode |
| `dim` | `int` (property) | Vector dimension |
| `backend` | `str` (property) | `"onnxruntime"` |
| `info()` / `stats()` | `ModelDesc` / `Stats` | Descriptor and usage statistics |
| `close()` | `None` | Release resources (idempotent); works with `with` |

Errors are `LembedError` subclasses (missing directory or file: `IOError`;
`.gguf` path: `UnsupportedError`; registry download failure: `DownloadError`).

#### Example

```python
from libembedding import MultiVectorEmbedding, maxsim

with MultiVectorEmbedding("/path/to/answerai-colbert-small-v1") as mv:
    (q,) = mv.query_embed("what is the capital of France?")   # (32, 96)
    docs = list(mv.passage_embed(["Paris is the capital of France.",
                                  "Bananas grow in tropical climates."]))
    scores = [maxsim(q, d) for d in docs]                      # first is highest
```

For many documents, `embed_ragged()` returns a `(data, offsets)` pair (one float32
`(total, dim)` array and `n + 1` int64 offsets) that `maxsim_batch(q, (data, offsets))`
hands to the native code with no copy at all; passing a list of matrices forces a
concatenation on every call (about 90 MB for 1000 long documents).

```python
    data, offsets = mv.embed_ragged(texts)       # document i = data[offsets[i]:offsets[i + 1]]
    scores = maxsim_batch(q, (data, offsets))    # zero copy
```

#### Performance

Measured against pylate (same model, same lengths 32 / 300) on an i7-1065G7, 3 rounds:
`MultiVectorEmbedding` is 1.14 to 1.5 times faster at batch 32 (short documents 242
against 160 texts/s, SciFact 10.8 against 9.5), up to 3 times faster at batch 1, loads the
model in 0.9 s against 8.5 s and uses 247 to 493 MB against 553 to 1021 MB. MaxSim scores
agree with pylate (Spearman 1.0000). The `maxsim` kernel is single-threaded: on long
documents (236 vectors) it is slower than numpy/BLAS. Protocol and limits:
`benchmarks/multivector/results.md`.

---

### Reranker

Scores and sorts documents by relevance to a query (cross-encoder).

On ONNX, query and document are encoded as a real sequence pair following the `tokenizer.json` `post_processor` (pair template, special tokens, `token_type_ids`) with `longest_first` truncation; BERT style `[CLS] q [SEP] d [SEP]` when none is declared. On GGUF (llama.cpp) the pair is built from the vocabulary's special tokens (BERT: `[CLS] q [SEP] d [SEP]`; XLM-R: `<s> q </s></s> d </s>`), same truncation.

#### Constructor

```python
Reranker(
    model_name="BAAI/bge-reranker-base",
    provider="cpu",
    device_id=0,
    cache_dir=None,
    max_length=0,
    threads=0,
    batch_size=256,
    offline=False,
    show_download_progress=True,
    backend="auto",
    quantization=None,
    cache_size=0,
    num_threads=None,  # deprecated
)
```

**Available models:**

| HuggingFace name | Description |
|------------------|-------------|
| `BAAI/bge-reranker-base` | BGE Reranker base (default) |
| `BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3` | Multilingual BGE Reranker v2 |
| `jinaai/jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en` | Jina Reranker v1 turbo |
| `jinaai/jina-reranker-v2-base-multilingual` | Jina Reranker v2 base multilingual |
| `jinaai/jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en-quantized` | Jina Reranker v1 turbo English (INT8) |

> **`quantization=` does not change the reranker weights.** Unlike
> `TextEmbedding`, it does not resolve a registry entry: it only sets ONNX
> Runtime session options. To get INT8, pass the **name** of the quantized entry
> — `jinaai/jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en-quantized`, which points at
> `onnx/model_quantized.onnx` of the `jinaai/jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en` repo.
> Unlike text models, a quantized reranker entry has a `model_name` **distinct**
> from its FP32 version, which is precisely why no per-mode resolution exists
> there today.

#### Methods and properties

| Member | Type | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `rerank(query, documents, batch_size=0)` | `list[RerankResult]` | Returns results sorted by descending score |
| `batch_size` | `int` (property) | Configured batch size |
| `name` | `str` (property) | Model name |
| `backend` | `str` (property) | `"onnxruntime"` or `"llama.cpp"`: with `backend="auto"` a model without ONNX files may be served by its GGUF counterpart, and this is where that switch shows |
| `provider` | `str` (property) | Execution provider actually in use (`"cpu"` for llama.cpp) |
| `info()` | `ModelDesc` | Model descriptor |
| `stats()` | `Stats` | Runtime usage statistics |
| `close()` | `None` | Release resources |
| `list_supported_models()` | `list[ModelInfo]` | (static) List reranker models |
| `auto(profile="balanced", **kwargs)` | `Reranker` | (static) Create a Reranker auto-configured by profile |

#### Example

```python
from libembedding import Reranker

reranker = Reranker("BAAI/bge-reranker-base")
ranked = reranker.rerank(
    "What is deep learning?",
    [
        "Deep learning uses neural networks",
        "The weather is sunny today",
        "Neural networks are inspired by biological brains",
    ],
)

for r in ranked:
    print(f"doc[{r.index}] score={r.score:.4f}")
```

---

## Data types

### TextEmbeddingPool

Pool of ONNX sessions for inter-session parallelism. See [performance_tuning.html](performance_tuning.html).

```python
class TextEmbeddingPool:
    model_name: str
    workers: int = 0                  # number of sessions (0 = auto-detect)
    threads_per_worker: int = 1       # threads per session
    batch_size: int = 256
    provider: str = "cpu"
    offline: bool = False
    show_download_progress: bool = True
    cache_dir: str | None = None
    max_length: int = 0
    dim: int = 0
    pooling: str = "mean"
    autotune: bool = False            # auto-tune all parameters
    autotune_texts: list[str] = None  # corpus for autotune
    autotune_max_samples: int = 100   # max sample size
    cache_size: int = 0               # LRU cache per worker (0 = disabled)
    quantization: str | None = None   # quantization mode
```

**Methods:**

| Member | Type | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `embed(texts)` | `np.ndarray` | Embed texts in parallel |
| `num_workers` | `int` (property) | Number of active workers |
| `dim` | `int` (property) | Embedding dimension |
| `close()` | `None` | Release resources |

### TuningResult

Result of autotune for a model.

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TuningResult:
    workers: int
    threads: int
    batch_size: int
    throughput_docs_sec: float
    latency_ms: float
    memory_mb: float
```

### ModelSelectionResult

Result of automatic model selection.

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ModelSelectionResult:
    model_code: str
    model_name: str
    dim: int
    workers: int
    threads: int
    batch_size: int
    throughput_docs_sec: float
    latency_ms: float
    memory_mb: float
    score: float
```

### Stats

Runtime usage statistics for an embedding context.

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Stats:
    texts_embedded: int    # total texts processed
    batches_run: int       # total ONNX batches executed
    avg_latency_ms: float  # average latency per embed() call (ms)
    cache_hits: int = 0    # LRU cache hits (0 if disabled)
    cache_misses: int = 0  # LRU cache misses (0 if disabled)
```

Available via the `.stats()` method on `TextEmbedding`, `Reranker`, `ImageEmbedding`, and `SparseTextEmbedding`.

### EmbeddingCache

Thread-safe LRU cache for dense embeddings. Can be used standalone or via the `cache_size` parameter of constructors.

```python
from libembedding import EmbeddingCache

cache = EmbeddingCache(capacity=4096, ttl_seconds=0, dim=0)
cache.put("text", np.array([...], dtype=np.float32))
vec = cache.get("text")  # np.ndarray | None
cache.clear()
```

| Method | Returns | Description |
|--------|---------|-------------|
| `put(text, vec)` | `None` | Store an embedding |
| `get(text, dim=None)` | `np.ndarray \| None` | Look up a cached embedding by text key |
| `clear()` | `None` | Remove all entries |
| `capacity` | `int` | Maximum cache capacity |
| `current_size` | `int` | Current number of entries |
| `close()` | `None` | Release underlying C resources |

### Global functions

| Function | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| `autotune(model_name, full=False, texts=None, max_sample_size=100)` | Auto-tune a model. Returns `TuningResult`. `texts` supplies a measurement corpus (sampled down to `max_sample_size`); `None` uses a synthetic corpus. |
| `autotune_unified(task="embedding", model_name=None, *, full=False, objective=None, texts=None, max_latency_ms=0.0, min_tokens=0, max_sample_size=100)` | **Recommended.** Unified auto-tune for the four tasks (`"embedding"`, `"reranking"`, `"image"`, `"sparse"`). Returns `UnifiedTuningResult`. See [Unified Tuning](#unified-tuning). |
| `clear_autotune_cache_unified(task, model_name=None)` | Clear one task's autotune cache for a model (`None` = every model of the task). |
| `sparse_autotune(model_name, *, full=False)` | Legacy: auto-tune sparse embedding (threads × batch). Returns `SparseTuningResult`. |
| `image_autotune(model_name, *, full=False)` | Legacy: auto-tune image embedding. Returns `ImageTuningResult`. |
| `reranker_autotune(model_name, *, full=False, objective="balanced")` | Legacy: auto-tune reranker. Returns `RerankerTuningResult`. |
| `reranker_auto_config(model_name, target_latency_ms=500, objective="balanced")` | Auto-config reranker for a latency budget. |
| `reranker_auto_config_profile(profile="balanced", model_name=...)` | Auto-config reranker using a profile. |
| `reranker_autotune_constrained(model_name, *, full=False, objective="balanced", min_tokens=64, max_latency_ms=500.0)` | Legacy: auto-tune with constraints. |
| `clear_reranker_autotune_cache(model_name=None)` | Clear reranker autotune cache. |
| `auto_select_model(use_case="balanced")` | Select best model. Returns `ModelSelectionResult`. |
| `clear_autotune_cache(model_name=None)` | Clear the text autotune cache (with `None`, the **whole** autotune directory). |
| `cache_path()` | Get autotune cache file path. |
| `cosine_similarity(a, b)` | Cosine similarity between two vectors |
| `dot_product(a, b)` | Dot product between two vectors |
| `euclidean_distance(a, b)` | L2 distance between two vectors |
| `maxsim(query, doc)` | MaxSim (ColBERT) score: sum over `query` rows of the max dot product with `doc` rows (2-D float32 matrices) |
| `maxsim_batch(query, docs)` | One MaxSim score per document (`docs`: sequence of float32 matrices, or a `(data, offsets)` pair passed without copy, see `MultiVectorEmbedding.embed_ragged`), in input order |
| `detect_backend(model_name, backend="auto")` | Detect which backend to use |

---

## Similarity Functions

Compare two embedding vectors natively without instantiation overhead.

```python
from libembedding import cosine_similarity, dot_product, euclidean_distance
import numpy as np

a = np.array([0.1, 0.2, 0.3], dtype=np.float32)
b = np.array([0.4, 0.5, 0.6], dtype=np.float32)

sim = cosine_similarity(a, b)
dp = dot_product(a, b)
dist = euclidean_distance(a, b)
```

## Unified Benchmark

Unified comparison of ONNX and llama.cpp backends with auto-tuning.

| Class/Constant | Description |
|------------------|-------------|
| `Benchmark` | Benchmark with auto-tuning and multi-backend comparison |
| `BenchmarkResult` | Result for a model x backend |
| `ComparisonResult` | Comparison results with recommendation |
| `Metrics` | Benchmark metrics (throughput, latencies, memory) |
| `HardwareInfo` | Detected hardware information |
| `CorpusType` | Corpus categories (SHORT, MEDIUM, LONG, MIXED, MULTILINGUAL, EDGE_CASES) |
| `Objective` | Optimization objectives (LATENCY, THROUGHPUT, BALANCED, MEMORY) |

```python
from libembedding import Benchmark, Objective, detect_backend, cache_path, clear_cache

# Backend detection
backend = detect_backend("BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")

# Benchmark
bench = Benchmark()
result = bench.autotune("path/to/model.gguf", "llama.cpp", objective=Objective.BALANCED)
comparison = bench.compare_all(onnx_path="model.onnx", gguf_path="model.gguf")

# Utilities
path = cache_path()
clear_cache()
```

---

### Unified Tuning

The unified auto-tuner provides a single API for optimizing all task types (text embedding, sparse, image, reranking). It runs on `lembed_autotune_ex()` on the C side.

```python
from libembedding import autotune_unified, UnifiedTuningResult

result = autotune_unified(
    "reranking",
    "jinaai/jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en-quantized",
    objective="latency",                      # default: "throughput" (embedding, sparse, image), "balanced" (reranking)
    texts=[*documents, query],                # reranking: the LAST text is the query
    max_latency_ms=300.0,                     # p95 budget per tuning call (0 = none)
    min_tokens=128,                           # reranking only
)
print(result.threads, result.batch_size, result.max_tokens)
```

When no configuration fits `max_latency_ms`, the best unconstrained configuration is returned: compare `result.p95_latency_ms` with the budget. `texts` is rejected for `"image"`. For `"sparse"`, `top_k`, `min_weight` and `storage_format` are not tuned: they reflect the model defaults. Details, grids, methodology and measurements: [Performance](performance_tuning.html#unified-auto-tuning-api).

#### UnifiedTuningResult

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class UnifiedTuningResult:
    task: str               # "embedding", "reranking", "image", or "sparse"
    threads: int
    batch_size: int
    workers: int            # embedding only
    max_tokens: int         # reranker only
    throughput_docs_sec: float
    latency_ms: float       # per call; amortised per text for "embedding"
    p95_latency_ms: float
    memory_mb: float        # estimate (RSS delta), not an absolute peak
    top_k: int = 0          # sparse: model default (not tuned)
    min_weight: float = 0.0 # sparse: same
    storage_format: int = 0 # sparse: same
```

### SparseTuningResult

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SparseTuningResult:
    top_k: int
    min_weight: float
    storage_format: int
    threads: int
    batch_size: int
    throughput_docs_sec: float
    latency_ms: float
    memory_mb: float
```

### ImageTuningResult

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ImageTuningResult:
    threads: int
    batch_size: int
    throughput_docs_sec: float
    latency_ms: float
    memory_mb: float
```

### RerankerTuningResult

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RerankerTuningResult:
    threads: int
    batch_size: int
    max_tokens: int
    throughput_docs_sec: float
    latency_ms: float
    p95_latency_ms: float
    memory_mb: float
```

### SparseEmbedding

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SparseEmbedding:
    indices: np.ndarray  # int32 — token IDs
    values:  np.ndarray  # float32 — token weights
```

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RerankResult:
    index: int   # index of the document in the input list
    score: float # relevance score (higher = more relevant)
```

### ModelInfo

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ModelInfo:
    model_name:    str   # e.g. "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5"
    model_code:    str   # HF repo code, e.g. "Xenova/bge-small-en-v1.5"
    model_file:    str   # e.g. "onnx/model.onnx"
    description:   str
    dim:           int   # embedding dimension
    max_tokens:    int   # max token length
    pooling:       str   # "cls" or "mean"
    quantization:  str   # "none", "static", "dynamic"
    download_size_mb: int = -1  # download size in MB, -1 if unknown
```

### ModelDesc

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ModelDesc:
    name: str
    dimension: int
    max_length: int
    pooling: str   # "cls" or "mean"
    num_threads: int
    batch_size: int
    provider: str = "cpu"          # requested provider ("llama" for every GGUF context)
    device_id: int = 0
    quantization: str = "none"
    cache_size: int = 0
    backend: str = ""              # "onnxruntime", "llama.cpp" or "ggml-sparse"
    effective_provider: str = ""   # provider actually in use: "cpu", "cuda", ...
```

`backend` and `effective_provider` are appended last with a default value, so
code building a `ModelDesc` positionally keeps working. `info()` always fills
them.

### Effective backend and provider

Every model reports which runtime serves it and which provider it really runs
on (`backend` / `provider` properties, `info().backend` /
`info().effective_provider` fields):

| `backend` | Meaning | `provider` |
|---|---|---|
| `"onnxruntime"` | ONNX model | `"cpu"`, or the requested provider if it could be attached |
| `"llama.cpp"` | Dense or reranker GGUF model | `"cpu"`; `"gpu"` when `llama_n_gpu_layers != 0` and ggml has a GPU device (none in the shipped builds) |
| `"ggml-sparse"` | SPLADE GGUF model (in-house ggml runtime) | `"cpu"` |

**Explicitly requested but unavailable provider: error.** `"cpu"` is the
default and there is no "auto" provider, so any other value is an explicit
request. When it cannot be attached (build without CUDA, DirectML or
TensorRT, CoreML outside Apple, `"llama"` on an ONNX model, unknown value),
construction raises `UnsupportedError` with the reason, instead of running on
the CPU without saying so. The check happens before the model is parsed.

```python
from libembedding import TextEmbedding
from libembedding.exceptions import UnsupportedError

try:
    model = TextEmbedding("BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5", provider="cuda")
except UnsupportedError as e:
    print(e.detail)  # "Execution provider CUDA was requested but is not available: ..."

model = TextEmbedding("BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")
print(model.backend, model.provider)  # onnxruntime cpu
```

**`Reranker(backend="auto")` switch to GGUF: kept, but visible.** When the
requested model has no ONNX files and a GGUF counterpart exists, the reranker
is served by llama.cpp: `reranker.backend` is then `"llama.cpp"`, and
`LIBEMBEDDING_VERBOSE=1` writes the switch to stderr.

---

## Error handling

All exceptions inherit from `LembedError`. The `LlamaError` subclass is raised for llama.cpp/GGUF backend failures:

```python
from libembedding import LembedError
from libembedding.exceptions import (
    InvalidArgumentError,
    OutOfMemoryError,
    OnnxRuntimeError,
    TokenizerError,
    DownloadError,
    IOError,
    ModelNotFoundError,
    UnsupportedError,
    BatchSizeError,
    LlamaError,
)
```

**Hierarchy:**

```
LembedError (Exception)
├── InvalidArgumentError   — NULL pointer or out-of-range enum
├── OutOfMemoryError       — allocation failure
├── OnnxRuntimeError       — ONNX Runtime error
├── TokenizerError         — tokenizer loading/encoding error
├── DownloadError          — download failure
├── IOError                — file I/O error
├── ModelNotFoundError     — unknown model
├── UnsupportedError       — feature disabled at compile time, or registry model above the mobile limit (Android/iOS)
├── BatchSizeError         — incompatible batch size
└── LlamaError             — llama.cpp/GGUF backend error
```

`LlamaError` provides:
- `status_code` (`int`) — `LEMBED_ERROR_LLAMA`
- `message` (`str`) — `"llama.cpp backend error"`
- `detail` (`str`) — technical detail from llama.cpp

#### Example

```python
from libembedding import TextEmbedding
from libembedding.exceptions import ModelNotFoundError, OnnxRuntimeError

try:
    model = TextEmbedding("nonexistent/model")
except ModelNotFoundError as e:
    print(f"Model not found: {e.detail}")
except OnnxRuntimeError as e:
    print(f"ONNX error: {e.detail}")
except LembedError as e:
    print(f"libembedding error [{e.status_code}]: {e.message}")
```

Each exception provides:
- `status_code` (`int`) — C error code
- `message` (`str`) — generic message (e.g. `"ONNX Runtime error"`)
- `detail` (`str`) — technical detail (e.g. ORT error message)

## Utility functions

```python
import libembedding

# List models by category
libembedding.list_text_models()      # list[ModelInfo]
libembedding.list_sparse_models()    # list[ModelInfo]
libembedding.list_image_models()     # list[ModelInfo]
libembedding.list_reranker_models()  # list[ModelInfo]

# Size limit for registry models (MB): 150 on Android and iOS, 0 (no limit)
# elsewhere. On mobile, list_*_models() only returns models within the limit.
# See Platforms.
libembedding.mobile_model_limit_mb()  # int
```

See [Platforms](platforms.html) for the mobile limit (`UnsupportedError`,
`LIBEMBEDDING_ALLOW_LARGE_MODELS`, `LIBEMBEDDING_FORCE_MOBILE_MODEL_LIMIT`).

### Auto-tuning workers (C API)

```c
#include <libembedding/worker_autotune.h>

lembed_worker_config_t cfg = lembed_detect_optimal_workers();
printf("Physical cores: %d\n", cfg.physical_cores);
printf("Optimal workers: %d\n", cfg.optimal_workers);
printf("Optimal threads: %d\n", cfg.optimal_threads);

int workers = lembed_recommended_workers_for_model("/path/to/model.gguf");
```

### Embedding modes (C API)

```c
#include <libembedding/embedding_mode.h>

lembed_embedding_mode_t mode = LEMBED_MODE_BALANCED;
lembed_text_model_t model = lembed_recommended_model_for_mode(mode);
const char* mode_str = lembed_mode_to_string(mode);  // "balanced"
```

### Embedding cache (C API)

```c
#include <libembedding/embedding_cache.h>

lembed_cache_config_t cfg = lembed_cache_config_default();
cfg.capacity = 4096;
cfg.ttl_seconds = 3600;  // 1 hour

lembed_cache_t* cache = lembed_cache_create(&cfg);
float* vec = NULL;
int dim = 0;
if (lembed_cache_get(cache, "Hello world", &vec, &dim)) {
    // cache hit
} else {
    // cache miss : calculate the embedding then store it
    float embedding[384];
    // ... calculation ...
    lembed_cache_put(cache, "Hello world", embedding, 384);
}

lembed_cache_free(cache);
```

### Effective backend and provider (C API)

Two accessors per context type. They return static strings (never freed), or
`NULL` when the context is `NULL`. No field is added to `lembed_model_desc_t`:
its size (ABI) does not change.

| Function | Values |
|---|---|
| `lembed_text_embedding_backend_name(ctx)` | `"onnxruntime"`, `"llama.cpp"` |
| `lembed_text_embedding_effective_provider(ctx)` | `"CPU"`, `"CUDA"`, `"CoreML"`, `"DirectML"`, `"TensorRT"`; llama.cpp: `"CPU"`, or `"GPU"` when layers are offloaded to a ggml GPU device |
| `lembed_sparse_text_embedding_backend_name(ctx)` | `"onnxruntime"`, `"ggml-sparse"` |
| `lembed_sparse_text_embedding_effective_provider(ctx)` | as for ONNX; `"CPU"` for `"ggml-sparse"` |
| `lembed_reranker_backend_name(ctx)` | `"onnxruntime"`, `"llama.cpp"` |
| `lembed_reranker_effective_provider(ctx)` | as for ONNX; `"CPU"` for llama.cpp |
| `lembed_image_embedding_backend_name(ctx)` | `"onnxruntime"` |
| `lembed_image_embedding_effective_provider(ctx)` | as for ONNX |

Provider names use the spelling of `lembed_execution_provider_name()`.

```c
lembed_text_options_t opts = lembed_text_options_default();
opts.provider = LEMBED_PROVIDER_CUDA;
lembed_text_embedding_t* ctx = NULL;
lembed_status_t s = lembed_text_embedding_create(&opts, &ctx);
if (s == LEMBED_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED) {
    /* "Execution provider CUDA was requested but is not available: ..." */
    fprintf(stderr, "%s\n", lembed_last_error());
    opts.provider = LEMBED_PROVIDER_CPU;
    s = lembed_text_embedding_create(&opts, &ctx);
}
printf("%s on %s\n", lembed_text_embedding_backend_name(ctx),
       lembed_text_embedding_effective_provider(ctx));  /* onnxruntime on CPU */
```

**Behaviour change (decision D3).** A provider other than
`LEMBED_PROVIDER_CPU` that cannot be attached to the ONNX Runtime session makes
the create function fail with `LEMBED_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED` and a message in
`lembed_last_error()`, instead of silently falling back to the CPU.
`LEMBED_PROVIDER_CPU` is the default of every options struct and there is no
"auto" provider, so any other value is an explicit request. This also applies
to `LEMBED_PROVIDER_LLAMACPP` given to an ONNX model and to an out-of-range
value. The GGUF paths (llama.cpp, `ggml-sparse`) ignore the provider as before.

The reranker `LEMBED_BACKEND_AUTO` switch (ONNX model not found, matching GGUF
entry) is kept: `lembed_reranker_backend_name()` then returns `"llama.cpp"`
and `LIBEMBEDDING_VERBOSE=1` logs the switch.

### Similarity (C API)

```c
#include <libembedding/similarity.h>

float a[] = {0.1f, 0.2f, 0.3f};
float b[] = {0.4f, 0.5f, 0.6f};
int dim = 3;

float sim = lembed_cosine_similarity(a, b, dim);
float dp  = lembed_dot_product(a, b, dim);
float dist = lembed_euclidean_distance(a, b, dim);
```

## DOCUMENT (EN): Available models
Source: https://github.com/dorel14/libembedding/blob/main/docs/en/models.md
Rendered: https://dorel14.github.io/libembedding-ng/en/models.html

# Available models

libembedding ships with a registry of pre-configured models. You can list them dynamically from Python:

> **Android and iOS:** only light models (≤ 150 MB) load by name, and
> `list_*_models()` returns only those. See
> [Platforms — mobile model limit](platforms.html).

> **Licence column:** the licence was read on each model's HuggingFace card
> (survey of 2026-11-14). A downloaded model keeps its own licence, independent of
> libembedding's MIT licence. When the repository actually downloaded is a conversion
> with no licence tag (Xenova, Qdrant, onnx-community…), it inherits the licence of the
> original model, shown in parentheses.

```python
import libembedding

for m in libembedding.list_text_models():
    print(f"{m.model_name:45} dim={m.dim:<5} {m.pooling} quant={m.quantization}")
```

## Text models (44 models)

> **Truncation length.** Without an explicit `max_length`, an ONNX text model truncates
> at the `max_seq_length` of its `sentence_bert_config.json`, like SentenceTransformers
> and FastEmbed: 256 for `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, 128 for `all-MiniLM-L12-v2` and both
> `paraphrase-multilingual-*` models, 384 for `all-mpnet-base-v2`, 512 for BGE, E5, MxBai
> and Arctic, 8192 for BGE-M3, GTE, Nomic, Jina and ModernBERT. Priority: explicit
> `max_length`, then `sentence_bert_config.json` when it sits in the model directory
> (local directory or cache), then the registry value (`max_tokens`, shown by
> `list_text_models()`), then `max_position_embeddings` of `config.json` for a local
> directory. These models used to truncate at 512: vectors of texts longer than 256
> (resp. 128, 384) tokens change and now line up with SentenceTransformers.

| `BAAI/bge-base-en-v1.5` | 768 | CLS | none | MIT |

### EmbeddingGemma (Google)

| HuggingFace name | Dim | Pooling | Quantization | Context | Licence |
|------------------|-----|---------|--------------|---------|---------|

> **Warning:** the project accepts only MIT-compatible licences. The Gemma licence is
> not an OSI licence (the `google/embeddinggemma-300m` repository is gated; the model is
> downloaded through `onnx-community/embeddinggemma-300m-ONNX`). It is therefore refused
> by default (`LEMBED_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED`, before any download);
> `LIBEMBEDDING_ALLOW_RESTRICTED_LICENSES=1` loads it anyway, once you have read its
> licence. It leaves the registry in 2.0.

| `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-s` | 384 | CLS | none | Apache-2.0 |
| `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-s` | 384 | CLS | dynamic | Apache-2.0 |
| `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-m` | 768 | CLS | none | Apache-2.0 |
| `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-m` | 768 | CLS | dynamic | Apache-2.0 |
| `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-m-long` | 768 | CLS | none | Apache-2.0 |
| `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-m-long` | 768 | CLS | dynamic | Apache-2.0 |
| `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-l` | 1024 | CLS | none | Apache-2.0 |
| `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-l` | 1024 | CLS | dynamic | Apache-2.0 |

> **Note:** Variants suffixed with `_Q` use quantization (static or dynamic) to reduce model size — 2 to 4x on dense text embeddings, per the [quantization benchmark](performance_tuning.html#quantization-fp32-vs-int8) — at the cost of slight accuracy loss. The throughput effect depends on the model and on the weights' actual format, and must be measured, not assumed. Two traps: the `_Q` suffix and the "quantized" label often mean **FP16**, not INT8 — initializers can be `FLOAT16`, which is the case for all four `Qdrant/*-onnx-Q` entries. On the reference CPU (Intel Core i7-1065G7, ONNX Runtime 1.29.0), dynamic INT8 came out **1.7x faster** than FP32 on MiniLM and **2.0x** on BGE, with 3.9x less disk and 1.8-2.1x less peak RAM; the BGE `_Q` entry, being FP16, came out **9.5x slower**. Benchmark your own model before choosing.

## GGUF models (llama.cpp backend)

GGUF models are loaded by file path or HuggingFace repo + file (not by enum). They use
the llama.cpp backend. The registry below (`lembed_list_gguf_models()`, source:
`include/libembedding/detail/gguf_registry_impl.hpp`) lists the files that were checked.

| Registry name | Original model | GGUF repository | Quant. | Dim | Size | Licence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Snowflake-XS-Q4` (default) | `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-xs` | `ChristianAzinn/snowflake-arctic-embed-xs-gguf` | Q4_K_M | 384 | ~20 MB | Apache-2.0 |
| `MiniLM-L6-Q4` | `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` | `second-state/All-MiniLM-L6-v2-Embedding-GGUF` | Q4_K_M | 384 | ~20 MB | Apache-2.0 |
| `MiniLM-L6-Q8` | `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` | `second-state/All-MiniLM-L6-v2-Embedding-GGUF` | Q8_0 | 384 | ~24 MB | Apache-2.0 |
| `Snowflake-S-Q4` | `snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-s` | `ChristianAzinn/snowflake-arctic-embed-s-gguf` | Q4_K_M | 384 | ~28 MB | Apache-2.0 |
| `E5-small-Q4` | `intfloat/e5-small-v2` | `ChristianAzinn/e5-small-v2-gguf` | Q4_K_M | 384 | ~28 MB | MIT |
| `GIST-small-Q4` | `avsolatorio/GIST-small-Embedding-v0` | `ChristianAzinn/gist-small-embedding-v0-gguf` | Q4_K_M | 384 | ~28 MB | MIT |
| `BGE-Reranker-v2-M3` (reranker, default) | `BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3` | `smarttasks/bge-reranker-v2-m3-GGUF` | Q4_K_M | — | ~438 MB | Apache-2.0 |
| `BGE-Reranker-Base` (reranker) | `BAAI/bge-reranker-base` | `sinjab/bge-reranker-base-Q4_K_M-GGUF` | Q4_K_M | — | ~219 MB | MIT (conversion tagged Apache-2.0) |
| `BGE-Reranker-Large` (reranker) | `BAAI/bge-reranker-large` | `sinjab/bge-reranker-large-Q4_K_M-GGUF` | Q4_K_M | — | ~407 MB | MIT (conversion tagged Apache-2.0) |
| `Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B` (reranker) | `Qwen/Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B` | `sinjab/Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B-Q4_K_M-GGUF` | Q4_K_M | — | ~396 MB | Apache-2.0 — **not supported yet** (decoder-only, `LEMBED_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED` at create), planned for 1.17 |
| `Jina-Reranker-v1-Turbo-En` (reranker) | `jinaai/jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en` | `sinjab/jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en-Q8_0-GGUF` | Q8_0 | — | ~36 MB | Apache-2.0 |

Any other GGUF file can still be loaded by path; the registry only records files that were checked.

Browse the full registry at runtime:

```python
import libembedding
# Note: GGUF models are listed via lembed_list_gguf_models() in C
```

```c
#include <libembedding/gguf_registry.h>

const lembed_gguf_model_info_t* models;
int count;
lembed_list_gguf_models(&models, &count);
for (int i = 0; i < count; i++) {
    printf("%-24s dim=%-4d quality=%.3f\n",
           models[i]->name, models[i]->dim, models[i]->quality_mteb);
}
```

## Compatibility matrix (reference validation)

A model is *validated* only once libembedding reproduces a reference. The reference
is the **same ONNX file** run with Python `onnxruntime`, fed with the ids, attention
mask and token types of HuggingFace `tokenizers`, then pooled and normalised as the
registry entry says. Weights and runtime are shared, so the gap measured here comes
from libembedding's tokenizer, input packing and pooling only. The test is
`python/tests/test_model_reference.py` (marker `network`, skipped when the model is
not cached or `onnxruntime`/`tokenizers` are missing); the report is regenerated with
`python python/tests/model_reference.py --json report.json`.

Corpus: the 54 texts of `tests/data/tokenizer/corpus.json` (English, accented Latin,
Vietnamese, Greek, Cyrillic, Arabic, Hebrew, Devanagari, CJK, Korean, Thai, emoji,
punctuation, combining marks, full-width) plus a text of more than 512 tokens.
Truncation is set to 512 on both sides. Measured with the WordPiece normalisation fix
of PR #62 (0 id mismatches against HF on the corpus).

Status on the minimum cosine: **validated** >= 0.999, **degraded** 0.99 to 0.999,
**broken** < 0.99. Measured on 2026-11-22, after the Unigram (PR #68), byte-level BPE
(PR #79) and `Whitespace` BPE (PR #97) tokenizers; quantized siblings in the same row.

| Model | Tokenizer family | Min / mean cosine (all) | English min | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| all-MiniLM-L6-v2, L12-v2 | WordPiece | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| bge-small-en-v1.5, bge-base-en-v1.5 | WordPiece | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| nomic-embed-text-v1.5 | WordPiece | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| mxbai-embed-large-v1 (quantized) | WordPiece | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| gte-base-en-v1.5, gte-large-en-v1.5 (quantized) | WordPiece | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| snowflake-arctic-embed-xs / s / m / m-long / l | WordPiece | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| Splade_PP_en_v1 (sparse, cosine of the vectors) | WordPiece | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2 | Unigram (SentencePiece) | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| multilingual-e5-small | Unigram (SentencePiece) | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| clip-vit-base-patch32 (text) | BPE byte-level | 1.000 / 1.000 | 1.000 | validated |
| jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en (reranker, Pearson of the scores; quantized 0.99996) | BPE (not byte-level, `Whitespace`) | 1.000 | n/a | validated |

Reading: **every measured model matches the reference** on the whole multilingual
corpus, whatever the tokenizer family (WordPiece, Unigram, byte-level BPE, `Whitespace`
BPE). The reranker (ONNX and GGUF) encodes a real pair, like HF (GGUF builds it from
the vocabulary's special tokens); its reference is the raw logit over 8 pairs,
Russian and Chinese included.

Not measured (not in the local cache or over 560 MB; the family comes from the
architecture, not from the `tokenizer.json`): all-mpnet-base-v2, bge-large-en-v1.5,
bge-small/large-zh-v1.5, nomic-embed-text-v1 and jina-embeddings-v2 (WordPiece),
bge-m3, multilingual-e5-base/large, paraphrase-multilingual-mpnet-base-v2 and the
XLM-R rerankers (Unigram), modernbert-embed-large (byte-level BPE), embeddinggemma-300m
(SentencePiece), the image models and the GGUF models. These are **not validated**.

## Querying model information

### From Python

```python
import libembedding

# List all models in a category
text_models = libembedding.list_text_models()
sparse_models = libembedding.list_sparse_models()
image_models = libembedding.list_image_models()
reranker_models = libembedding.list_reranker_models()
multivector_models = libembedding.list_multivector_models()

# Inspect a specific model
for m in text_models:
    if m.model_name == "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5":
        print(f"dim={m.dim}, max_tokens={m.max_tokens}, pooling={m.pooling}")
        break
```

### From C

```c
#include <libembedding/model_registry.h>

const lembed_model_info_t* models;
int count;
lembed_list_text_models(&models, &count);

for (int i = 0; i < count; i++) {
    printf("%-40s dim=%-4d %s pooling  %s\n",
           models[i].model_name,
           models[i].dim,
           models[i].pooling == LEMBED_POOLING_CLS ? "CLS" : "Mean",
           models[i].description);
}
```

## DOCUMENT (EN): Performance Tuning

Rendered: https://dorel14.github.io/libembedding-ng/en/performance_tuning.html

# Performance Tuning

This guide covers libembedding's advanced performance features: session pooling, auto-tuning, automatic model selection, llama.cpp/GGUF tuning, bucketing, LRU cache, and FAST/BALANCED/QUALITY modes.

## Table of Contents

1. [Session Pool (EmbeddingPool)](#session-pool-embeddingpool)
2. [Auto-Tuning](#auto-tuning)
3. [Automatic Model Selection](#automatic-model-selection)
4. [llama.cpp / GGUF Performance](#llamacpp--gguf-performance)
5. [Benchmarks](#benchmarks)
6. [Best Practices](#best-practices)
7. [CPU Requirements](#cpu-requirements)

---

## CPU Requirements

The x86-64 wheels and release binaries run on any x86-64 CPU; no AVX or AVX2 is assumed:

- libembedding's own code (tokenizer, pooling, SPLADE pooling, similarity) is compiled for the
  x86-64 baseline; CI runs the unit tests under Intel SDE as Nehalem (SSE4.2), Sandy Bridge (AVX),
  Haswell (AVX2), Skylake-X (AVX-512) and Ice Lake (AVX-512 VNNI);
- GGUF models run on ggml, which ships one CPU library per instruction-set level (`ggml-cpu-x64` …
  `ggml-cpu-alderlake`) and loads the best one for the machine at run time;
  `libembedding.ggml_cpu_features()` (C: `lembed_ggml_cpu_features()`) names it. Record it with any
  GGUF benchmark;
- ONNX models run on ONNX Runtime, which selects its own kernels at run time.

The Linux aarch64 wheel (`manylinux_2_31_aarch64`) runs on 64-bit ARM Linux with glibc 2.31 or
later: Raspberry Pi 3/4/5 under 64-bit Raspberry Pi OS (bullseye, bookworm), Ubuntu 20.04 and later,
AWS Graviton, Ampere. ggml ships eight ARM CPU libraries (`ggml-cpu-armv8.0_1` …
`ggml-cpu-armv9.2_2`) and loads the best one at run time. CI builds it in a `manylinux_2_28`
container and tests it on GitHub's ARM runners; it has not been tested on a physical Raspberry Pi
yet. On 32-bit Raspberry Pi OS (armv7l, `manylinux_2_35_armv7l` wheel), ggml has a single CPU
library, built for ARMv7-A with NEON (no run-time selection). On Linux, HTTPS downloads use the system
CA bundle; `SSL_CERT_FILE` or `CURL_CA_BUNDLE` override it.

The macOS wheel is arm64 (Apple Silicon, macOS 14 or later), built natively. For a local build that
will never leave the machine, `-DLIBEMBEDDING_ARCH_AVX2=ON` compiles libembedding's own code for
AVX2. The hot paths are in ONNX Runtime and ggml, and on an i7-1065G7 the difference stayed within
noise (dense, sparse ONNX, sparse GGUF: 0.98–1.01×).

---

## Session Pool (EmbeddingPool)

For small Transformer architectures (MiniLM, BGE-small, E5-small), **inter-session** parallelism (multiple independent ONNX sessions) is more efficient than **intra-session** parallelism (ORT threading).

### When to use it

| Scenario | Recommendation |
|----------|----------------|
| < 100 embeddings | Single `TextEmbedding` is sufficient |
| > 100 embeddings | `TextEmbeddingPool` recommended |
| Production / high throughput | `TextEmbeddingPool` + `autotune=True` |

### Usage

```python
from libembedding import TextEmbeddingPool

# Pool with 8 workers (independent ONNX sessions)
pool = TextEmbeddingPool(
    "Qdrant/all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx",
    workers=8,               # number of parallel sessions
    threads_per_worker=1,    # 1 thread per session (avoids contention)
    batch_size=64,
    offline=True,
)

embeddings = pool.embed(texts)
pool.close()
```

### Performance gain

| Configuration | Docs/s (short texts) | Speedup |
|---------------|------------------------|---------|
| 1 session × 4 threads | ~100 | 1.0x |
| 4 workers × 1 thread | ~265 | 2.6x |
| **8 workers × 1 thread** | **~360** | **3.6x** |

> **Golden rule**: `workers × threads ≤ CPU core count`

**Count physical cores, not logical threads.** With hyperthreading (SMT), a
4-core processor reports 8 logical threads, but two threads on one core share its
vector units. Measured on an i7-1065G7 (4 cores, 8 logical threads) with ONNX
Runtime: going from 4 to 8 threads loses 20 to 30 % of throughput instead of
gaining any. Keep `workers × threads` at or below the number of **physical
cores**.

Defaults when `threads` is not set (`threads=0`):

- **ONNX**: the library does not set a thread count and lets ONNX Runtime
  choose. On the measurement machine, that choice is already right (4).
- **GGUF (llama.cpp)**: a fixed 4 threads, whatever the processor. On a machine
  with 2 physical cores, or with several workers, pass `threads=` explicitly.
- **Autotune and `TextEmbeddingRouter`**: they start from the detected number of
  physical cores, so they already follow this rule.

---

## Auto-Tuning

Auto-tuning automatically finds the optimal configuration (workers, threads, batch_size) for your hardware and corpus.

### Simple usage

```python
from libembedding import TextEmbeddingPool

# Autotune with synthetic corpus (default)
pool = TextEmbeddingPool(
    "Qdrant/all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx",
    autotune=True,           # enable auto-tuning
    offline=True,
)
# → benchmark on the first call (~13 s in QUICK on an i7-1065G7 4C/8T,
#   indicative: depends on the CPU and the model), then instant cache
```

### Autotune with your corpus (recommended)

For more accurate results, provide a sample of your actual texts:

```python
# Use a representative sample of your data
sample_texts = your_csv["text_column"].head(1000).tolist()

pool = TextEmbeddingPool(
    "Qdrant/all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx",
    autotune=True,
    autotune_texts=sample_texts,    # your corpus
    autotune_max_samples=100,        # samples 100 representative texts
    offline=True,
)
```

### Autotune cache

Results are cached by machine + model, in a **single store** shared by the four
tasks: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\libembedding\autotune` (`~/.cache/libembedding/autotune`
outside Windows). Text sits at the root; `reranker/`, `sparse/` and `image/` have
their own subdirectory. The file name encodes the hardware fingerprint and a
short hash of the variant (workload, objective, mode).

| Event | Behavior |
|-------|----------|
| First call | Benchmark + save cache |
| Same machine + model + workload | Cache hit (a few ms) |
| Change CPU/ORT/library version/model | Cache miss → re-benchmark |
| Same model, different workload | Cache miss (the workload fingerprint is part of the key) |
| Same workload, different objective, mode or constraint | Cache miss |

A cache entry is reused only when **every** identity dimension matches: model,
hardware and software fingerprint, workload fingerprint, objective, mode and
constraints (p95 budget, `min_tokens`). A corrupt or truncated file is treated as
a miss, never as a result. Writes are atomic (temporary file then rename), so an
interruption cannot leave a partial result behind. Sparse, image and constrained
results are now cached too (previously a repeated sparse or image call re-ran a
95 to 170 s tuning). Older text-tuner entries are invalidated: they had been
measured on the default model whatever model was requested.

Tune on your own corpus:

```python
from libembedding import autotune

result = autotune(
    "Qdrant/all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx",
    texts=my_documents,   # sampled down to max_sample_size (100 by default)
)
```

```python
from libembedding import clear_autotune_cache

# Clear cache for a model (all its variants: synthetic and custom)
clear_autotune_cache("Qdrant/all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx")

# Clear all cache
clear_autotune_cache()
```

### Full API

```python
# TextEmbedding with autotune
model = TextEmbedding(
    "Qdrant/all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx",
    autotune=True,
    autotune_texts=sample_texts,
    autotune_max_samples=100,
    offline=True,
)

# TextEmbeddingPool with autotune
pool = TextEmbeddingPool(
    "Qdrant/all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx",
    autotune=True,
    autotune_texts=sample_texts,
    autotune_max_samples=100,
    offline=True,
)
```

### Unified Auto-Tuning API

`autotune_unified()` is the single entry point for the four tasks
(`"embedding"`, `"reranking"`, `"sparse"`, `"image"`). It runs on
`lembed_autotune_ex()` on the C side ([C reference](c_api/autotuner.html)).

```python
from libembedding import autotune_unified, clear_autotune_cache_unified

# Defaults: QUICK, synthetic corpus, objective "throughput"
# (embedding, sparse, image) or "balanced" (reranking)
result = autotune_unified("embedding", "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")

# Reranker on your real data: the LAST text is the query.
# p95 budget of 300 ms for one whole request, never truncate below 128 tokens.
result = autotune_unified(
    "reranking",
    "jinaai/jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en-quantized",
    objective="latency",
    texts=[*candidate_documents, user_query],
    max_latency_ms=300.0,
    min_tokens=128,
)
print(result.threads, result.batch_size, result.max_tokens)
if result.p95_latency_ms > 300.0:
    print("budget not reachable: best unconstrained configuration returned")

# Purge one task's cache for a model (None = every model of that task)
clear_autotune_cache_unified("reranking", "jinaai/jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en-quantized")
```

Parameters: `full=False` (QUICK) or `True` (FULL); `objective` (`"latency"`,
`"throughput"`, `"balanced"`, `"memory"`, `None` = task default); `texts`
(custom workload, sampled down to `max_sample_size=100`; rejected for `"image"`);
`max_latency_ms` (p95 budget per tuning call, `0` = none); `min_tokens`
(reranking only). `model_name` has a default only for `"embedding"` and
`"reranking"`.

**Objectives.** `latency` minimizes the tail cost P50 + ½(P95 − P50);
`throughput` maximizes throughput; `balanced` averages the two normalized costs;
`memory` minimizes the RSS delta (16 MB floor).

**Budget and fallback.** Only configurations whose p95 fits in `max_latency_ms`
are eligible. When none fits, the best **unconstrained** configuration is returned
(no error): compare `result.p95_latency_ms` with the budget. For the reranker the
fallback keeps the `min_tokens` floor. A tuning call is one request over the whole
workload (the documents of a reranking query, or the full batch); the latencies in
the result are **per call**, except for `"embedding"` where they are amortised
**per text**.

**`min_tokens` and truncation.** The reranker tuner chooses `max_tokens`
(256/64 in QUICK; 256/128/64/32 in FULL), which **truncates documents**: this is a
quality/speed trade-off, not a pure optimization. `min_tokens` sets the acceptable
floor. On equal performance (within 5 %), the configuration that truncates least
wins.

**What the tuner deliberately does not set.** For sparse, `top_k`, `min_weight`
and `storage_format` change the vectors produced, not only the speed: they are no
longer chosen by the tuner (only threads × batch are). `UnifiedTuningResult` still
exposes those three fields, filled with the model defaults, for information.

**Grids.** Text: splits of the *P* physical cores between workers and threads
(1×P, 2×P/2, P×1 in QUICK; + 1×logical and 4×P/4 in FULL) × batch (32 in QUICK;
16/32/64/128 in FULL), on a synthetic corpus of 32 (QUICK) or 256 (FULL) texts.
Reranker: threads (physical and logical cores in QUICK; + 1, 2, P/2 in FULL) ×
`max_tokens` × batch. Sparse (ONNX) and image: threads × batch.

**Sparse GGUF runtime.** `sparse_autotune()` (and `lembed_sparse_autotune()`,
whose result struct is unchanged; `lembed_sparse_autotune_v2()` also returns
`workers`, `max_batch_tokens` and `from_cache`) also accept the sparse GGUF registry name (`"SPLADE-PP-En-v1-GGUF"`, already
cached locally: the tuner never downloads) or a path ending in `.gguf`; the
backend is chosen by the name (the `backend` option exists only for the reranker and dense text, not for sparse). The grid is
threads × workers (graphs sharing the weights) × batch × `max_batch_tokens`
(token budget per batch, 512 by default). `top_k` and `min_weight` are never
tuned. The default configuration is point 0 of the grid and is returned when no
candidate beats it, so the result is never worse than the default on the same
benchmark. Entries live in the same tune cache (`sparse/` sub-directory), keyed
by the file path (or registry name) plus the file size and modification time; a
second call returns the stored configuration without benchmarking
(`from_cache=True`). Apply the result with `threads=`, `workers=` and
`set_max_batch_tokens()`:

```python
r = sparse_autotune("model.gguf")
m = SparseTextEmbedding("model.gguf", threads=r.threads, workers=r.workers)
m.set_max_batch_tokens(r.max_batch_tokens)
```

### Measurement methodology

The four tuners share one engine:

- **Protocol.** QUICK: 1 warmup + 3 timed calls per configuration; FULL: 2 warmups
  + 10 calls. Every call is timed individually (monotonic clock); P50 and P95 are
  derived from them.
- **Racing.** A configuration whose **first two** timed calls both exceed 2.5 × the
  best P50 seen so far is stopped without finishing its calls (except for the
  `memory` objective, which does not rely on latency).
- **Session reuse.** Grid points that differ only by batch size share one ONNX
  Runtime session instead of rebuilding it.
- **Unit-free costs.** Every term is divided by the best value observed among the
  eligible configurations; a cost is "how many times worse than the best" (lower
  is better). Constraints (p95 budget, `min_tokens`) filter *before* normalization,
  so an ineligible configuration cannot distort the references.
- **5 % tie rule.** Costs within 5 % of the best are treated as a tie, resolved
  toward the preferred configuration (first grid point: fewer sessions, less
  truncation, smaller batch). The choice therefore does not flip on measurement
  noise.
- **Memory.** `memory_mb` is an **estimate**: RSS growth across "create + warmup",
  not an absolute peak. Peak RSS is a per-process high-water mark: do not read it
  as the exact footprint of one configuration.

### bench_autotune measurements

Measured with `benchmarks/bench_autotune.cpp` (public C API only), QUICK mode,
Release build, **i7-1065G7 (4C/8T)**, before → after the engine rework (commit
`e263a90` and following):

| Task (model) | Cold tuning | Repeated call (cache) | Throughput of chosen config vs library defaults |
|--------------|-------------|-----------------------|-------------------------------------------------|
| text (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`) | 22.7 → 12.6 s | ~10–15 ms (already cached before) | ×1.77 |
| reranker (`jina-reranker-v1-turbo-en`) | 144 → 47 s | ~10–15 ms (already cached before) | ×3.26 |
| sparse (`Splade_PP_en_v1`) | 96 → 33 s | 95.6 s → 11 ms | ×1.01 |
| image (`clip-ViT-B-32-vision`) | 166 → 28 s | 168 s → 13 ms | ×1.01 |

How to read this table:

- Throughput is measured on an **evaluation workload distinct** from the tuner's,
  against the library defaults.
- **Text ×1.77**: the earlier ×1.23 was measured on the *wrong model* (the old
  tuner always measured the default model).
- **Reranker ×3.26**: obtained mostly by **truncating to 64 tokens**. It is a speed
  gain paid for with context seen by the model; use `min_tokens` if ranking quality
  matters.
- **Sparse ×1.01 and image ×1.01**: the defaults are already close to optimal on
  this machine; the tuner confirms it, it does not manufacture a gain.
- **Observed evaluation noise is 6 to 14 %** on this laptop: do not present small
  differences as significant. These durations depend on the CPU, the model and the
  workload: they are indicative, not guarantees.

To reproduce (the Windows CMake generator is multi-config; models must already be
in the local cache, nothing is downloaded):

```powershell
cmake -S . -B build -DLIBEMBEDDING_BUILD_BENCHMARKS=ON
cmake --build build --config Release --target bench_autotune
build/benchmarks/Release/bench_autotune.exe --task text   # text | rerank | sparse | image
# options: --model <code> (the task's model), --runs <n> (default 5, median reported)
```

The JSON report goes to stdout, progress to stderr.

### llama.cpp / GGUF Auto-Tuning

When building with llama.cpp support, the unified benchmark and autotuner can compare ONNX vs llama.cpp:

```python
from libembedding import Benchmark, CorpusType, Objective

bench = Benchmark()
comparison = bench.compare_all(
    onnx_path="/path/to/model.onnx",
    gguf_path="/path/to/model.Q4_K_M.gguf",
    corpus=CorpusType.MIXED,
    objective=Objective.BALANCED,
)
print(comparison.recommendation.backend)  # "onnx" or "llama.cpp"
```

## Automatic Model Selection

To automatically choose the best model for your hardware and use case:

```python
from libembedding import auto_select_model, TextEmbeddingPool

# Auto-select best model for your hardware + use case
result = auto_select_model("balanced")  # "speed", "quality", or "balanced"

print(f"Best model: {result.model_name}")
print(f"Config: {result.workers} workers × {result.threads} threads")
print(f"Throughput: {result.throughput_docs_sec:.0f} docs/s")

# Create pool with optimal config
pool = TextEmbeddingPool(
    result.model_code,
    workers=result.workers,
    threads_per_worker=result.threads,
)
embeddings = pool.embed(texts)
```

### Use cases

| Use case | Recommendation |
|----------|----------------|
| Real-time, latency critical | `"speed"` |
| Semantic search, max quality | `"quality"` |
| General production | `"balanced"` (default) |

### Candidates and the model cache

The selection runs a QUICK autotune on each candidate. It only measures
candidates that are already in the model cache: it downloads nothing, and it
leaves nothing behind (no model folder is created for a candidate that is
missing, which is skipped). To let the selection consider a model, download it
first.

| Candidate | Cache directory | Download |
|---|---|---|
| `Qdrant/all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx` | `models--Qdrant-all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx` | 90 MB |
| `Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` (INT8) | `models--Xenova-all-MiniLM-L6-v2` | 23 MB |
| `Xenova/all-MiniLM-L12-v2` | `models--Xenova-all-MiniLM-L12-v2` | 133 MB |
| `Xenova/bge-small-en-v1.5` | `models--Xenova-bge-small-en-v1.5` | 133 MB |
| `Xenova/bge-base-en-v1.5` | `models--Xenova-bge-base-en-v1.5` | 436 MB |
| `Xenova/paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2` | `models--Xenova-paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2` | 470 MB |
| `intfloat/multilingual-e5-small` | `models--intfloat-multilingual-e5-small` | 470 MB |

The cache is the default directory: `LIBEMBEDDING_CACHE_DIR`, then
`FASTEMBED_CACHE_DIR`, then `~/.cache/libembedding`.

---

## llama.cpp / GGUF Performance

For CPU-bound embedding workloads with small BERT-style models, the optimal configuration is:

### Recommendations

| Parameter | Recommended value | Reason |
|-----------|-------------------|--------|
| `threads` | **1** per session | Avoids contention on small BERT models |
| `workers` / `sessions` | **physical_cores × 2** (max 8) | Near-linear scaling until saturation |
| `batch_size` | 8-32 | Depends on average text length |
| `batch_strategy` | `LENGTH_BUCKET` | Reduces padding for heterogeneous corpora |

### Auto-tuning workers

```python
from libembedding import TextEmbedding

model = TextEmbedding(
    "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5-GGUF",
    auto_workers=True,      # auto-detect optimal session count
    cache_size=4096,        # optional LRU cache
)
```

### Preset modes

```python
# FAST : speed priority (all-MiniLM-L6-v2, 384 dim, ~90 MB)
model = TextEmbedding.from_mode("fast")

# BALANCED : speed/quality compromise (default)
model = TextEmbedding.from_mode("balanced")

# QUALITY : quality priority
model = TextEmbedding.from_mode("quality")
```

### LRU Cache

```python
# Enable LRU cache with 4096 entries
model = TextEmbedding(
    "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5-GGUF",
    cache_size=4096,
)
```

### Baseline i7-1065G7 (MiniLM-L6-v2 Q4_K_M)

| Sessions | Threads | Docs/s |
|----------|---------|--------|
| 1 | 1 | 41.8 |
| 1 | 4 | 72.1 |
| 4 | 1 | 105.9 |
| **6** | **1** | **125.9** |
| 8 | 1 | 129.4 |
| 6 | 2 | 87.6 |

**Conclusion**: beyond 2 sessions, `threads=1` is always faster. Intra-session multithreading degrades performance on BERT embedding models.
